1. &quot;PGKHY&quot; - Rossmann fold motif for FAD/NAD binding

This protein is most likely human dihydrolipoamide dehydrogenase (DLD, also known as E3 component).

Now, regarding the subunit organization of dihydrolipoamide dehydrogenase (E3):
- Dihydrolipoamide dehydrogenase (DLD) is known to function as a homodimer
- The enzyme contains two identical subunits, each binding FAD and NAD
- The active site is formed at the interface between the two subunits
- Each subunit provides part of the active site, including the redox-active disulfide from one subunit and the FAD from the other

Looking at the structural biology of DLD:
- DLD from various organisms (pig, human, E. coli) all form homodimers
- The homodimer is the functional unit
- The active site is at the interface between the two subunits

So the answer should be A. Homodimer.

Let me verify by checking other possibilities:
- Homotetramer: Some enzymes like pyruvate dehydrogenase E2 form multimers, but E3/DLD is a homodimer
- Monomer: DLD is not a monomer; the active site requires two subunits
- Heterodimer: DLD is a homodimer, not a heterodimer

The answer is A. Homodimer.
